I guess I do it to stop the worrying, but... like I said, it just makes me slower. I don't feel as... free to just design.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with colleagues or friends outside of work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 40 / designer / bachelors\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"I’m still getting my work done, but I’ve started missing internal deadlines and holding back on bolder design ideas because I’m afraid I’ll overlook something and damage my chances of moving toward a creative director role.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um... I don't know if I think something really bad will happen. I just... like I was just saying, if I don't go back and re-open the file and check the layers again, I'll just keep worrying that it's wrong.

I'd rather not be thinking about them.\n\nDoctor: Do these nagging thoughts cause you a lot of distress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I'd say a lot. It's... mostly just frustrating, I guess. I wouldn't really call it distress. Just... annoying.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts about work pop into your mind suddenly, without you expecting them?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose. Like I said before... they just kind of happen. I'll be doing something else and suddenly my mind is just... circling back to whether I missed a mistake in a layout, or... the client files. So, yeah... they just pop up.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts affecting your ability to do your daily activities or are you able to carry on with your tasks as usual?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm still getting everything done, mostly. I mean, I'm doing my tasks. But... I guess I've been missing some internal deadlines lately. And... I don't know, I haven't been pitching the bolder ideas as much. I just... I worry I'll miss something in the details, and... well, I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, so I don't want to mess that up. But I'm still working.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything repeatedly, like checking your work multiple times, to try and get rid of these nagging thoughts?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check things a bit more than I used to. Before I send anything off, I'll... open the file again. Just to look at the layers. I'll save it again, just to be sure. And... sometimes I just sit there and go over every design choice in my head one last time. It's not like it takes up my whole day or anything, but... it slows me down. Makes it harder to just... design.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your work multiple times helps reduce your worry about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um... a little, maybe. Like I was just saying with... reopening everything and comparing the layers... I guess it makes me feel better for a second. But mostly it just... it just makes me slower. I feel a lot less free, creatively, when I'm stuck doing all that. So... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: When you check your work multiple times, are you trying to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Like I said, I just... I re-open the files and compare the layers and re-save them. And mentally walk through everything. I just don't want to make a mistake that... ruins something. It doesn't take over my whole day, but... yeah. It just slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checking behaviors to relieve the worry about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah. Like I was just saying, I feel like I have to. Before I submit the work, I just... I have to re-open the file. Compare the layers, re-save it. Go through every choice in my head one more time. I guess I do it to stop the worrying, but... like I said, it just makes me slower. I don't feel as... free to just design.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with colleagues or friends outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... not really with friends, I guess. I keep it to myself mostly. With colleagues... I don't know. I'm still getting my work done, but... I have been missing some internal deadlines lately. Just because it takes me longer to finish. And... I guess I've been holding back a bit on, like, bolder ideas. I just... I don't want to overlook something and mess up. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and... I just don't want to damage my chances. So I play it safe.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to spend time with friends or family because of these thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Like I just said, not really with friends. Or family. I keep it to myself around them. It's mostly just... work. Like I was saying, I'm still getting my projects done, but... I'm missing those internal deadlines. And holding back on bolder design ideas. I just... I don't want to overlook something and damage my chances for that creative director role. So... yeah. It doesn't really affect my family.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these nagging thoughts as entering your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I definitely don't try to think about them. They just... show up. I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I've missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or... that I sent the wrong version to a client. And even when I can see it's probably fine, my mind just... keeps circling back to it. I can't really turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts feel like they're forced into your mind, even when you're trying not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I said, I don't try to think about them. They just... happen. I'll be trying to focus on something else, but I just keep getting those nagging thoughts about missing a tiny mistake in a layout, or... sending the wrong file. Even when I know it's fine, my mind just... keeps going back to it. It just forces its way in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these checking behaviors to neutralize or undo the thoughts about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yes. Um... like, before I submit my work now, I'll... I'll re-open the file. And I just sort of compare the layers, make sure everything is right, and then re-save it. Sometimes I just... I mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be sure. It doesn't take over my entire day or anything, but... it does make me slower. And less free, creatively. So... yeah, I guess I do it to feel better about those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried that if you don't check your work in this detailed way, something very bad will happen as a result of a mistake?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I think something really bad will happen. I just... like I was just saying, if I don't go back and re-open the file and check the layers again, I'll just keep worrying that it's wrong. \n\nSo I do all that... the re-saving, the mental walk-throughs of my choices... just to be certain. It's not like I think it'll be a catastrophe, it just... it slows me down, like I mentioned. I just prefer to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are necessary to counteract the thoughts about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Like I just said, I guess so. It just... helps me stop worrying about it. I don't really know how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are in line with your own beliefs and values?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure what you mean by that. My values? I just... I just want my designs to be right. I haven't really thought about it like that. They're just... thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts about making mistakes are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess? I mean, I've always just been careful with my work. I wouldn't really say it's... like, my identity or anything. It's just stuff that pops in my head while I'm designing. I haven't really analyzed it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't feel as... free to just design.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with colleagues or friends outside of work?\n\nPatient: Um... not really with friends, I guess. I keep it to myself mostly. With colleagues... I don't know. I'm still getting my work done, but... I have been missing some internal deadlines lately. Just because it takes me longer to finish. And... I guess I've been holding back a bit on, like, bolder ideas. I just... I don't want to overlook something and mess up. I really want to move toward a creative director role eventually, and... I just don't want to damage my chances. So I play it safe.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to spend time with friends or family because of these thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Like I just said, not really with friends. Or family. I keep it to myself around them. It's mostly just... work. Like I was saying, I'm still getting my projects done, but... I'm missing those internal deadlines. And holding back on bolder design ideas. I just... I don't want to overlook something and damage my chances for that creative director role. So... yeah. It doesn't really affect my family.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these nagging thoughts as entering your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I definitely don't try to think about them. They just... show up. I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I've missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or... that I sent the wrong version to a client. And even when I can see it's probably fine, my mind just... keeps circling back to it. I can't really turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts feel like they're forced into your mind, even when you're trying not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I said, I don't try to think about them. They just... happen. I'll be trying to focus on something else, but I just keep getting those nagging thoughts about missing a tiny mistake in a layout, or... sending the wrong file. Even when I know it's fine, my mind just... keeps going back to it. It just forces its way in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these checking behaviors to neutralize or undo the thoughts about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yes. Um... like, before I submit my work now, I'll... I'll re-open the file. And I just sort of compare the layers, make sure everything is right, and then re-save it. Sometimes I just... I mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be sure. It doesn't take over my entire day or anything, but... it does make me slower. And less free, creatively. So... yeah, I guess I do it to feel better about those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried that if you don't check your work in this detailed way, something very bad will happen as a result of a mistake?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I think something really bad will happen. I just... like I was just saying, if I don't go back and re-open the file and check the layers again, I'll just keep worrying that it's wrong. \n\nSo I do all that... the re-saving, the mental walk-throughs of my choices... just to be certain. It's not like I think it'll be a catastrophe, it just... it slows me down, like I mentioned. I just prefer to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are necessary to counteract the thoughts about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Like I just said, I guess so. It just... helps me stop worrying about it. I don't really know how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are in line with your own beliefs and values?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure what you mean by that. My values? I just... I just want my designs to be right. I haven't really thought about it like that. They're just... thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts about making mistakes are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess? I mean, I've always just been careful with my work. I wouldn't really say it's... like, my identity or anything. It's just stuff that pops in my head while I'm designing. I haven't really analyzed it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are something that you would normally think, or are they somehow foreign to your own way of thinking?\n\nPatient: I mean... Like I just said, not really with friends. Or family. I keep it to myself around them. It's mostly just... work. Like I was saying, I'm still getting my projects done, but... I'm missing those internal deadlines. And holding back on bolder design ideas. I just... I don't want to overlook something and damage my chances for that creative director role. So... yeah. It doesn't really affect my family.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe these nagging thoughts as entering your mind against your will?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I definitely don't try to think about them. They just... show up. I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I've missed some tiny mistake in a layout, or... that I sent the wrong version to a client. And even when I can see it's probably fine, my mind just... keeps circling back to it. I can't really turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these thoughts feel like they're forced into your mind, even when you're trying not to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I said, I don't try to think about them. They just... happen. I'll be trying to focus on something else, but I just keep getting those nagging thoughts about missing a tiny mistake in a layout, or... sending the wrong file. Even when I know it's fine, my mind just... keeps going back to it. It just forces its way in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing these checking behaviors to neutralize or undo the thoughts about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yes. Um... like, before I submit my work now, I'll... I'll re-open the file. And I just sort of compare the layers, make sure everything is right, and then re-save it. Sometimes I just... I mentally walk through every single design choice one more time. Just to be sure. It doesn't take over my entire day or anything, but... it does make me slower. And less free, creatively. So... yeah, I guess I do it to feel better about those thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried that if you don't check your work in this detailed way, something very bad will happen as a result of a mistake?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if I think something really bad will happen. I just... like I was just saying, if I don't go back and re-open the file and check the layers again, I'll just keep worrying that it's wrong. \n\nSo I do all that... the re-saving, the mental walk-throughs of my choices... just to be certain. It's not like I think it'll be a catastrophe, it just... it slows me down, like I mentioned. I just prefer to be sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are necessary to counteract the thoughts about making mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. Like I just said, I guess so. It just... helps me stop worrying about it. I don't really know how else to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are in line with your own beliefs and values?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure what you mean by that. My values? I just... I just want my designs to be right. I haven't really thought about it like that. They're just... thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts about making mistakes are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess? I mean, I've always just been careful with my work. I wouldn't really say it's... like, my identity or anything. It's just stuff that pops in my head while I'm designing. I haven't really analyzed it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are something that you would normally think, or are they somehow foreign to your own way of thinking?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know. They're just thoughts I have when I'm working. I wouldn't call them foreign, they're just... there. I haven't really thought about it that deeply.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts about making mistakes are in line with how you think you should be thinking?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know how to answer that. I don't think I have a specific way I'm supposed to be thinking. It's just... my brain working while I do my job. I don't really keep track of it like that."
